## Review

**8 out of 10, Strong.** Presentation quality, judged with the SlideDeck Co review heuristic (https://slidedeck.co/review-methodology/). The ask, the evidence and the merit of the idea are not rated.

Each slide carries one idea in a plain headline, and the argument builds in steps: the PC and smartphone installed-base charts (slides 3 to 6) set up S curves, the layered curve diagrams (slide 7 onward) follow, and orange divider slides then mark each section. The system holds from start to finish: dark slate ground, orange and blue accents, gold subtitles, consistent chart styling with sources, and full-bleed or half-slide photos that carry a point, such as the washing machine versus robot pair and the Wal-Mart photo on slide 60. Slides 7 and 26 are sparse but readable, with large type and good contrast. The weaknesses are that some slides lean on the speaker to supply meaning (slide 7 is mostly empty, and slides 49 and 50 are near-repeats), small footers and some axis labels are tiny, and a few diagram slides such as slides 5 and 6 repeat the same frame with only minor additions; this is acceptable for live delivery but weaker as a read-alone file.

**Worth keeping:** Keep the one-idea-per-slide discipline, the dark ground with orange and blue accents, the clean chart styling, and the orange section-divider questions that signal each new curve.

**Most useful repair:** Cut or merge the near-duplicate build slides (such as 5 and 6 and 49 and 50) and enlarge the footers and chart axis labels where they fall below comfortable reading size.

**What would change the score:** I would add a short on-slide takeaway to the sparsest diagram slides, such as slide 7, so they still communicate when read without the speaker.

_Confidence medium. 72 of 76 pages inspected on contact sheets, 4 at readable size. Reviewed Reviewed 2026-11-08. Model-assisted review (claude-sonnet-5-5)._

## Summary

The deck opens with a title slide and a speaker card for Benedict Evans, a partner at Andreessen Horowitz, presenting at the a16z Summit. It builds an argument in sections: first S curves, showing the PC installed base giving way to smartphones and the move from PC internet to mobile internet, then the scale and character of today's largest technology companies, then four emerging S curves to watch: AI, autonomy, mixed reality and cryptocurrency. Each section uses long runs of simple one-idea slides, with charts, diagrams and quotations rather than bullet lists. The talk closes on a return to the theme of innovation, a recap of the four emerging curves and a short thank-you slide. Slides worth seeing include the S curve charts that carry the first argument, the layered diagram of mobile and PC internet, and the single-quote slide that sets up the crypto comparison.

## Questions about this deck

**What does the Benedict Evans deck say about AI?**

Slide 29 lists AI as one of four emerging S Curves to consider, alongside autonomy, mixed reality and cryptocurrency. Slide 30 is a section slide titled AI, and slide 32 lists unhelpful ways to talk about AI.

**How is the Benedict Evans Ten Year Futures deck structured?**

The deck opens with a title slide and a speaker card for Benedict Evans, a partner at Andreessen Horowitz (slides 1 and 2). It moves through S curves of PC and mobile internet (slides 3 to 11), the scale of today's largest tech companies (slides 14 to 24), and then new S curves, starting with the question on slide 25.

**What does the Benedict Evans deck say about Microsoft's share of personal computing?**

Slide 21, titled But winners always look invulnerable, until they don't, shows a line chart of Microsoft's share of personal computing unit sales (PCs, Macs, Linux, smartphones and tablets) from 2000 to 2016 on a 0% to 100% scale.

**What quote from Marc Andreessen appears in the Benedict Evans deck?**

Slide 10 quotes Marc Andreessen: I arrived in Silicon Valley in 1994 and I thought I was too late and missed the whole thing.

**Layout**

Single-slide chart layout with no visible grid or columns. The title sits top-left at x 3.4%, y 6.1%, with the amber subtitle directly beneath it at x 3.5%, y 13.3%, both left-aligned. The x-axis year labels sit along the bottom at y 82%. The footer line sits at the bottom-left (x 2.9%) and the source line at the bottom-right (x 74.8%), both at y 95.5%. No slide number is visible.

**Visuals**

Chart: area-and-line chart, with no chart title inside the plot. Y axis is labelled 0, 1, 2, 3, 4 (unit: billions, bn, per the subtitle 'Global installed base (bn)'), range 0 to 4. X axis is labelled 1985, 1990, 1995, 2000, 2005, 2010, 2015 (years), spanning 1985 to about 2017. Series 1 'iOS & Android smartphones' is a filled area in #6C96B2, starting near 0 in 2008, about 0.03 in 2009, about 0.15 in 2010, about 0.4 in 2011, about 0.8 in 2012, about 1.6 in 2013, about 2.5 in 2015, about 2.85 in 2016 and about 3.2 in 2017 (all values estimated, none printed). No highlighted points. Legend at right: a #6C96B2 swatch labelled 'iOS & Android smartphones' above a #C2468A line swatch labelled 'PCs'. The x-axis baseline is a thin white horizontal rule. No gridlines, no tick marks, no data labels.

**Design**

Background: Solid colour #40484F (dark slate grey) covering the whole slide. Flat, solid dark slate background with no gradient, texture or shadow. Title in white (#FFFFFF) light geometric sans-serif (ProximaNova-Light), about 5.9% of slide height. Subtitle in amber (#FCB316) light sans-serif, about 4.0% of slide height. Axis and legend labels in white (#FFFFFF) Helvetica-Light style sans-serif: year labels about 3.5% of slide height, y-axis labels and legend text about 2.7-3.0%. Footer and source text is very small white-grey sans-serif, about 1.2% of slide height. Accent colours: amber #FCB316 for subtitle, blue-grey #6C96B2 for the area fill and legend swatch, magenta #C2468A for the PCs line. Lines are flat with no rounded caps; the PCs line stroke is about 0.5% of slide height. No corner radii, no shadows.

### Slide 5

**Review notes**

- Review: Each slide carries one idea in a plain headline, and the argument builds in steps: the PC and smartphone installed-base charts (slides 3 to 6) set up S curves, the layered curve diagrams (slide 7 onward) follow, and orange divider slides then mark each section.
- Review: The weaknesses are that some slides lean on the speaker to supply meaning (slide 7 is mostly empty, and slides 49 and 50 are near-repeats), small footers and some axis labels are tiny, and a few diagram slides such as slides 5 and 6 repeat the same frame with only minor additions; this is acceptable for live delivery but weaker as a read-alone file.

**Text**

S Curves / Global installed base (bn) / 6 / 5 / 4 / 3 / 2 / 1 / 0 / 1985 / 1990 / 1995 / 2000 / 2005 / 2010 / 2015 / Mobile users / iOS & Android / smartphones / PCs / ©2017 Andreessen Horowitz. Proprietary and confidential. / Source: Apple, Google, a16z, TMW, ITU, World Bank

**Layout**

Single full-bleed slide with left-aligned title and subtitle at the top-left margin (about 3.4% from the left). A wide line chart fills the middle, with its legend stacked in a column at the right edge of the plot. A thin copyright line sits bottom-left and a source line bottom-right, both at about 95.5% from the top. No slide number is visible.

**Visuals**

Chart: overlapping area and line chart titled by the slide subtitle 'Global installed base (bn)'. Y axis: 0 to 6 bn, labelled 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6 with gridlines absent. X axis: years 1985 to 2017, labelled 1985, 1990, 1995, 2000, 2005, 2010, 2015. Series 1, Mobile users: light blue-grey filled area (#A7C0D1) rising from about 0.0 in 1985 to about 0.5 in 2001, about 1.6 in 2005, about 2.7 in 2008, about 3.5 in 2010, about 4.4 in 2013, about 4.9 in 2016 and about 5.05 in 2017. Series 2, iOS & Android smartphones: mid blue filled area (#6C96B2) at about 0.0 until 2008, about 0.15 in 2010, about 0.4 in 2011, about 0.8 in 2012, about 1.5 in 2013, about 2.0 in 2014, about 2.45 in 2015, about 2.9 in 2016 and about 3.2 in 2017. Series 3, PCs: magenta line (#C44A8E), about 0.03 in 1985, about 0.4 in 1999, about 0.55 in 2002, about 1.0 in 2008, about 1.45 in 2011, about 1.5 peak in 2013 to 2014, falling to about 1.4 in 2017. No highlighted points. Legend: at the right of the plot, three entries top to bottom: light blue-grey swatch with 'Mobile users', mid blue swatch with 'iOS & Android smartphones', magenta line sample with 'PCs'. Baseline: thin white horizontal axis line at the zero level, spanning the plot width. Footer images: copyright and source lines rendered as small images of text. Bottom-right small image (81 by 52 px) is a logo-sized picture that is not legibly visible on the render.

**Design**

Background: Solid dark slate #40484F. Dark slate background (#40484F) with white (#FFFFFF) title and axis numerals, amber (#FCB316) subtitle, and light blue-grey and mid blue fills with a magenta line for data. Title in a light geometric sans-serif (Proxima Nova Light), about 5.9% of slide height. Subtitle in the same family, about 4.0%. Axis labels in a light Helvetica-style sans-serif, about 2.7% to 3.5% of slide height. Legend text in Helvetica Light about 3.0%. Flat fills with no gradients, shadows or rounded corners. Areas have no outlines. Line width of PCs series about 0.4% of slide height.

### Slide 6

**Review notes**

- Review: Each slide carries one idea in a plain headline, and the argument builds in steps: the PC and smartphone installed-base charts (slides 3 to 6) set up S curves, the layered curve diagrams (slide 7 onward) follow, and orange divider slides then mark each section.
- Review: The weaknesses are that some slides lean on the speaker to supply meaning (slide 7 is mostly empty, and slides 49 and 50 are near-repeats), small footers and some axis labels are tiny, and a few diagram slides such as slides 5 and 6 repeat the same frame with only minor additions; this is acceptable for live delivery but weaker as a read-alone file.

**Layout**

A single full-bleed chart on a dark slate field with no boxed panels. The title sits top-left at about 6% from the top and 3.4% from the left, with an amber subtitle below it. The chart occupies the middle of the slide, with the y-axis numbers at x about 5.3% and the x-axis year labels along a baseline at about 81% of the height. The legend is stacked at the right, starting at x about 84.5%. A footer row at about 95.5% of the height holds a left-aligned copyright line and a right-aligned source line. No slide number is shown.

**Visuals**

Chart: an overlapping area and line chart titled 'Global installed base (bn)'. Y-axis: 0 to 6, unit billions (bn), integer ticks 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, printed as text with the zero at the baseline. X-axis: years 1985 to 2017, labelled every five years (1985, 1990, 1995, 2000, 2005, 2010, 2015), with the last plotted point at about 2017. Series: (1) Mobile users, light blue #A7C0D1 filled area, rising from about 0 in 1985 to about 0.6 around 2001, about 1.6 around 2005, about 3.5 around 2010, about 4.6 around 2014, and about 5.05 in 2017. (2) iOS & Android smartphones, mid blue #6C96B2 filled area, near 0 until about 2009, about 0.8 around 2012, about 2.0 around 2014, and about 3.2 in 2017; values are estimates, none printed. (3) PCs, magenta line (about #C4478A), about 0.02 in 1985, about 0.4 in 1999, about 1.2 around 2009, peaking about 1.5 around 2013 to 2014, and about 1.4 in 2017; estimates. (4) Adults, green line (about #9BC53D), about 3.2 in 1985, about 3.5 around 1990, about 4.2 around 2000, about 4.9 around 2009, and about 5.5 in 2017; estimates. Legend: four entries at right, Mobile users (light blue filled swatch), iOS & Android smartphones (mid blue filled swatch), PCs (magenta line swatch), Adults (green line swatch). No highlighted points. Footer: copyright and source lines are rendered as small images of text.

**Design**

Background: Solid dark slate #40484F. Dark slate background #40484F with no gradient, texture or shadows. Title 'S Curves' in white #FFFFFF, light geometric sans-serif (ProximaNova-Light), about 5.9% of slide height, weight light. Subtitle in amber #FCB316, ProximaNova-Light, about 4.0% of slide height. Chart labels and legend in white #FFFFFF, Helvetica Light sans-serif, axis numbers about 2.7% of slide height, year labels about 3.5%, legend about 3.0%. Footer text is small, about 1.2% of height, white. Filled areas have no outlines; lines are about 0.4% of slide height thick with round ends. The x-axis baseline is a thin white line.

### Slide 7

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: A minimal two-curve diagram makes the core analogy readable at a glance without any text beyond the labels.
- Review: Each slide carries one idea in a plain headline, and the argument builds in steps: the PC and smartphone installed-base charts (slides 3 to 6) set up S curves, the layered curve diagrams (slide 7 onward) follow, and orange divider slides then mark each section.
- Review: Slides 7 and 26 are sparse but readable, with large type and good contrast.
- Review: The weaknesses are that some slides lean on the speaker to supply meaning (slide 7 is mostly empty, and slides 49 and 50 are near-repeats), small footers and some axis labels are tiny, and a few diagram slides such as slides 5 and 6 repeat the same frame with only minor additions; this is acceptable for live delivery but weaker as a read-alone file.
- What would change the score: I would add a short on-slide takeaway to the sparsest diagram slides, such as slide 7, so they still communicate when read without the speaker.

**Text**

S Curve follows S Curve
Mobile
internet
PC
internet
©2017 Andreessen Horowitz. Proprietary and confidential.

**Layout**

Single-canvas diagram slide with no grid or columns; the title sits top-left at about 3.4% from the left edge and 6.1% from the top, left-aligned. Two S-curve paths run across the lower-middle of the slide, with the copyright footer bottom-left at about 2.9% from the left edge and 95.5% from the top.

**Visuals**

Diagram with two circles and two curved arrows. Left: a dotted-outline circle labelled 'PC internet' (white bold text), centred near x 7.7–17.7%, y 61.2–79.0%, with a dotted blue-grey (#6C96B2) stroke. A dotted blue-grey curve enters from the bottom-left edge (about x 0%, y 90%) and rises into the circle's left side; a second dotted blue-grey line leaves the circle's right side and ends in a small blue-grey (#6C96B2) triangular arrowhead at about x 48–52%, y 64–67%, pointing right. Right: a solid orange (#D98228) ring labelled 'Mobile internet' (white bold text), centred near x 44.3–54.3%, y 54.5–72.3%, with an orange stroke of about 0.5% of slide width and no fill beyond the background. A solid orange curve starts at about x 21.8%, y 80% at the bottom-left, rises through the bottom of the Mobile circle, continues out of its top-right and curves upward to an orange triangular arrowhead at about x 76–78%, y 22–24%, pointing right. The orange curve passes behind the Mobile circle; the circle has a fill matching the background. Shapes have no shadows, gradients or rounded rectangles; the only curved and filled elements are the circles and arrowheads. Copyright footer is a small logo-free text line.

**Design**

Background: Solid flat colour #40484F (dark slate grey) covering the entire slide. Flat dark slate background #40484F with no gradients or textures. Title in white (#FFFFFF) ProximaNova Light sans-serif, regular weight, approx 5.9% of slide height, sentence case with capital S and C. Circle labels in white ProximaNova Bold sans-serif, approx 3.5% of slide height, centred over circles in two lines. Accents: orange #D98228 for the Mobile internet ring, curve and arrowhead; muted blue #6C96B2 for the PC internet dotted curves and arrowhead. Footer text is small white-grey sans-serif at about 1% of slide height. Lines are about 0.5% of slide width thick; PC circle uses a dotted stroke, Mobile circle a solid stroke.

### Slide 8

**Text**

Mobile now = PCs in 2005 / What can you build on top of the platform? / Ride-sharing, Instagram, Instacart… / SEO, SEM, Social… / Mobile / internet / PC / internet / ©2017 Andreessen Horowitz. Proprietary and confidential.

**Layout**

Single full-bleed slide with a left-aligned title block at top-left (x 3.4%, y 6.1%) and a subtitle directly beneath it. A two-stage diagram fills the middle and lower band, rising from lower-left to upper-right, with two labels above the arrow branches. The copyright footer sits bottom-left at y 95.5%; no page number or logo is visible.

**Visuals**

Diagram: a left circle labelled 'PC internet' with a dotted blue (#6C96B2) outline, and a larger-looking right circle labelled 'Mobile internet' with a solid orange (#D98228) outline. A dotted blue line enters from the bottom-left edge, passes through the PC circle, and branches into three upward dotted arrows and one right-pointing arrow, labelled 'SEO, SEM, Social…'. A solid orange curve starts at lower-left (about x 21.8%, y 84.8%), passes through the Mobile circle, and rises to a solid right-pointing arrowhead at about x 77.8%, y 40.4%. Three dotted orange upward arrows branch from that curve toward the label 'Ride-sharing, Instagram, Instacart…'. No chart, table or photograph.

**Design**

Background: Solid charcoal #40484F across the entire slide. Flat dark charcoal background with no gradient, shadow or texture. Title in light-weight white sans-serif (ProximaNova Light), about 5.9% of slide height, sentence case. Subtitle in light-weight amber sans-serif, about 4.0% of height. Diagram labels in light white sans-serif (about 3.0% of height); circle labels in bold white sans-serif (about 3.5% of height). Accents: orange #D98228 for the Mobile arrows and circle, steel blue #6C96B2 for the PC arrows and circle. Lines are thick round-dotted strokes for the branches and a solid stroke for the main curves; circles have no corner radius.

### Slide 9

**Text**

And the future is always boring when we get there
“Innovation is dead”
“Innovation is dead”
Mobile
internet
PC
internet
©2017 Andreessen Horowitz. Proprietary and confidential.

**Layout**

Single full-bleed slide with no grid or columns. The title sits top-left at about 6% from the top and 3.4% from the left edge, on one line. Two tall dark panels sit side by side in the lower-middle area, with their headings aligned to their top edges. A footer copyright line sits bottom-left at about 95.5% from the top. No slide number is visible.

**Visuals**

Diagram. Two tall dark rectangles, each labelled “Innovation is dead” at the top, with a dotted light-blue circle labelled “PC internet” at the lower left, a small dotted light-blue arrow running from that circle's right edge into the left panel, and a curved dotted light-blue line entering from the bottom-left edge of the slide into the PC circle. A solid orange circle labelled “Mobile internet” sits between the two panels, with a solid orange curve starting at the bottom of the left panel, passing up through the Mobile circle, and ending in an orange arrowhead pointing right, inside the right panel. Stroke widths are about 0.5% of slide height for the orange lines and about 0.4% for the dotted blue lines. No axes, legend or photographs.

**Design**

Background: Solid slate grey #40484F, full slide. Flat dark slate background with no gradient or texture. Title is light-weight sans-serif in white #FFFFFF, roughly 5.9% of slide height, with no capitals or letter-spacing. Panel headings and circle labels are bold sans-serif in white #FFFFFF, about 3.5% of slide height. Panels are #30363B with square corners and no shadow. Circles have no fill beyond the background colour #40484F. Orange accent #D98228 is used for the solid curve and circle outline. Dotted line and arrow are #6C96B2. Footer text is small light grey sans-serif at about 1.3% of slide height.

**Elements and positions** (back to front; x, y, width, height as % of the slide; type, colour and alignment in brackets)

- shape at x 0, y 0, w 100, h 100 [#40484F]: Full-slide background rectangle (Covers the entire slide with no border.)
- shape at x 17.8, y 24.7, w 19.5, h 64.4 [#30363B]: Left dark panel (Square corners, no border, no shadow, behind the diagram lines.)

- line at x 0, y 65.3, w 28.9, h 34.7 [#6C96B2]: Dotted light-blue line entering from the bottom-left edge of the slide, curving up into the PC internet circle (Dotted stroke about 0.4% of slide height, round dots, slight S-curve, starts at the slide's left edge near y 90%.)
- shape at x 7.7, y 61.2, w 10, h 17.8 [#40484F]: PC internet circle outline (Circle with dotted stroke #6C96B2, interior fill matches background, stroke about 0.4% of slide height, no fill colour difference.)

### Slide 11

**Text**

But there’s always something else coming / PC / internet / Mobile / internet / ? / ©2017 Andreessen Horowitz. Proprietary and confidential.

**Layout**

Single full-bleed slide with a left-aligned title at the top-left margin (about 3.4% from left, 6% from top) and three circular nodes arranged left to right in a rising diagonal flow across the middle and lower half. The footer copyright line sits bottom-left at about 3% margin, with the logo-style image at the bottom-left corner; there is no visible slide number.

**Visuals**

Diagram: three circles connected by curved and dotted arrows showing progression. Left circle (about 7.7% x, 61.2% y, 10% w, 17.8% h) is dark grey fill #40484F with a dotted light steel-blue outline #6C96B2 containing bold white text 'PC internet'; a dotted steel-blue line leaves its right edge at mid-height and ends in a small steel-blue triangular arrowhead pointing right at about x 27%, y 73%; a dotted steel-blue line also runs from the lower-left edge of the circle off the left edge of the slide, from about y 82% to the bottom-left corner at y 90%. Centre circle (about 44.3% x, 54.5% y, 10% w, 17.8% h) is dark grey #40484F with a solid orange #D98228 outline about 0.4% of slide width thick containing bold white text 'Mobile internet'; a thick orange curved arrow starts at the bottom-left of the slide around x 21.8%, y 85% and rises through the circle's bottom edge, out of its top-right edge, sweeping up and right to end in a solid orange triangular arrowhead at about x 77.5%, y 40.4%. Right circle (about 79.6% x, 48.7% y, 10% w, 17.8% h) is dark grey #40484F with a dotted steel-blue outline containing a large bold white question mark '?'; a dotted steel-blue line enters its left side from the left at about y 66% and another leaves its upper-right edge and runs up and off the top-right edge of the slide at about x 100%, y 33%. Footer image: small white copyright wordmark at bottom-left (about 2.9% x, 95.5% y, 20.3% w, 3.1% h), 305 by 26 pixels.

**Design**

Background: Solid dark grey #40484F covering the entire slide. Dark charcoal background #40484F throughout, flat with no gradient, texture or shadow. Title is a light-weight geometric sans-serif (ProximaNova-Light), white #FFFFFF, roughly 5.9% of slide height, sentence case, left-aligned. Circle labels are bold geometric sans-serif (ProximaNova-Bold), white #FFFFFF, about 3.5% of slide height, centred, two lines each. Accent colours: orange #D98228 for the primary mobile path (outline and arrow), steel blue #6C96B2 for the PC and unknown paths and dotted connectors. Line styles: solid thick orange curves for the main path; dotted steel-blue lines with round dots for secondary paths; circles have no fill other than the background colour. No corner radii, no shadows.

### Slide 12

**Text**

So, two innovation conversations today
‘What now?’ and ‘What next?’
What can we build on
the billion-scale
platforms we have
today?
What are the next
S Curves - what will
the next platforms be?
©2017 Andreessen Horowitz. Proprietary and confidential.

**Layout**

Two equal circles sit side by side in the lower two-thirds of the slide, centred on a horizontal midline at about 57% of slide height, with a double-headed arrow bridging the gap between them. The title sits top-left at about 3.4% left margin and 6% from the top, with the subtitle directly beneath it, both left-aligned. The copyright footer sits bottom-left at about 2.9% margin. There is no page number and no logo other than the footer line.

**Visuals**

Two outlined circles and one double-headed connector arrow, no photographs or charts. Left circle: orange outline (about #D9822B, roughly 0.3% of slide height stroke), no fill, centred at about x 25%, y 57%, spanning about x 7.0% to 43.4% and y 24.6% to 89.3%, containing the bold white text 'What can we build on / the billion-scale / platforms we have / today?'. Right circle: steel-blue outline (about #6393B5, same stroke weight), no fill, spanning about x 56.6% to 93.1% and y 24.6% to 89.3%, containing the bold white text 'What are the next / S Curves - what will / the next platforms be?'. Connector: thin dotted grey line (about #A8A8A8) running horizontally at y about 56.6% from the left circle's right edge (x 43.4%) to the right circle's left edge (x 56.6%), with solid filled grey triangular arrowheads at both ends pointing outward toward each circle. The footer is a small raster image of the copyright line in light grey-white text.

**Design**

Background: Solid slate grey #40484F. Flat dark slate background with no gradient, texture or imagery. Title is white (#FFFFFF) ProximaNova Light, a clean geometric sans-serif, regular weight, at about 5.9% of slide height. Subtitle is amber (#FCB316) ProximaNova Light at about 4.0% of slide height. Circle labels are white (#FFFFFF) ProximaNova Semibold at about 4.4% of slide height, centred, with tight leading (about 1.0 to 1.1 line spacing). Shapes use thin uniform outlines with no fills, no shadows, no rounded corners beyond the circular geometry. Footer is small light grey text at about 1% of slide height.

### Slide 13

**Text**

Massive experimentation around current platforms
New models and end-points
Bots? Voice? Smart speakers?
Wearables? Watches? Smart home?
TV shows? As marketing? Lock-in?
Social as pop culture, camera as
platform
VR? AR? Accessory or next platform?
Meta-conversation
Is there any white space left?
Can you even compete with
Google/Apple/Facebook/Amazon?
©2017 Andreessen Horowitz. Proprietary and confidential.

**Layout**

A full-bleed dark slate slide with a single title at top left, two amber column headings sitting on the dark header band, and a two-column body split by a right-pointing chevron divide. The left column is a grey panel with four stacked question groups and the right column is a lighter grey panel with two questions. The footer sits bottom left in the lower dark band with a margin of about 3% from the left edge. There is no page number and no slide number visible.

**Visuals**

Two-panel chevron graphic: a left panel in mid-dark grey #626465 and a right panel in lighter grey #838586 sit between y 30.1% and 89.4% of the slide height. The boundary between them is a chevron that points right, with a dark band #40484F about 2.5% of slide width wide running along the boundary, so the left panel's right edge notches inward and the right panel's left edge points left into it. The chevron tip is at about x 50% and y 60% of the slide. No photographs, charts, icons or logos other than the footer wordmark image.

**Design**

Background: Solid dark slate #40484F. Flat design with no gradients, shadows or corner radii. Background #40484F. Title in ProximaNova Light (geometric sans-serif, light weight), white #FFFFFF, about 5.9% of slide height, sentence case. Column headings in ProximaNova Semibold (sans-serif, semibold), amber #FCB316, about 4.4% of slide height. Body text in ProximaNova Regular (sans-serif, regular weight), white #FFFFFF, about 4.0% of slide height, sentence case with question marks, line spacing about 4.7% of slide height within a paragraph and about 8% between paragraphs. Shapes are plain flat fills with no outlines. Footer is a small light-grey wordmark image, about 3.1% of slide height tall.

### Slide 14

**Text**

Big scary tech giants / are nothing new… / ©2017 Andreessen Horowitz. Proprietary and confidential. / Source: Andy Hertzfield

**Layout**

Two-column split: a dark slate panel fills the left 35% of the slide and a full-height black-and-white photograph fills the right 65%. The title sits top-left in the panel at about 3.4% from the left edge and 6.4% from the top, left-aligned with no bullets. Footer lines sit along the bottom of the panel at about 95.5% of the height, the copyright at the left margin and the source line to its right. There is no slide number.

**Visuals**

One photograph, full-height and full-bleed within the right 65% of the slide, not boxed and with no overlay or tint. Subject: an IBM logo mounted on a dark, rough textured wall, with a man in a dark leather bomber jacket, open-collared light shirt and light trousers leaning against the wall beneath it. He raises his right arm with the index finger pointing up, mouth open as if speaking, and stands near the centre-right of the image. A thin dark horizontal line runs across the wall at about 70% of the image height. The IBM letters are the classic horizontal-striped white logo, spanning roughly 49% to 86% of the slide width and 9% to 35% of the height. Composition: the logo is in the upper half and the figure is in the lower half, with the figure's legs cropped by the bottom edge. Lighting is flat, even and monochrome. Dominant colours are charcoal grey and off-white. The photo is 976 by 845 pixels.

**Design**

Background is a solid slate panel #40484F on the left with the photograph on the right. Title text is white #FFFFFF in a light geometric sans-serif (Proxima Nova Light), weight light, sentence case, at about 5.9% of slide height, with line spacing of roughly 7.7% between baselines. Footer text is small light grey #C5CBD1 in a regular-weight sans-serif at about 1.5% of slide height. No accent colours, no shapes beyond the panel and photo, no shadows, gradients or rounded corners. The panel and photo meet at a hard vertical edge at 35% of the width.

Source: Bloomberg, BLS, a16z

**Layout**

A single full-width stacked area chart sits below a left-aligned title and subtitle block, with the plot running from about x 7.4% to 81% and the legend in a column at the right edge of the plot (x 84 to 90%). The title sits at the top-left at about y 6%, the subtitle directly beneath it at about y 13%, and the x-axis year labels form a row at about y 82%. The footer copyright line is at bottom-left (about y 95.5%) and the source line at bottom-right (about x 84%, y 95.5%), with no slide number.

**Visuals**

Chart: stacked area chart, titled 'Annual revenue ($bn, real)'. Y-axis printed values: '-' (zero, at baseline), 200, 400, 600, 800, with the printed axis label 'Thousands' rotated along the left edge; the subtitle states the unit as $bn, real. X-axis: years 1980 to 2016 labelled every five years (1980, 1985, 1990, 1995, 2000, 2005, 2010, 2015); the series ends at about 2016. Series stacked bottom to top: IBM (orange #D98228), Wintel (steel blue #6C96B2), GAFA (magenta #BE4681). No values are printed on the data; estimates: total about 8 in 1980, about 60 in 2000, about 250 at the 2008 peak and about 690 in 2016. IBM about 90 at 2008, about 80 in 2016; Wintel top about 220 in 2016 (about 130 thick); GAFA about 470 thick in 2016 (from about 220 to about 690). Legend: vertical list at right with square markers, top to bottom GAFA (magenta), Wintel (blue), IBM (orange), in white text; no highlighted points. Footer and source are rendered as small text images.

**Design**

Background: Solid dark slate grey #40484F. Dark charcoal slate background #40484F with no gradient or texture. Title and axis labels in white #FFFFFF, subtitle and accent in amber #FCB316. Typeface is a light geometric humanist sans (ProximaNova Light) for title and subtitle; axis labels and legend are a light Helvetica-style sans. Title about 5.9% of slide height, subtitle about 4.0%, axis labels about 3.5%, legend and y-axis labels about 3.0%, footer about 2.7%. Chart uses flat filled areas with no outlines, no shadows, no corner radii; a thin white baseline runs along the zero line.

©2017 Andreessen Horowitz. Proprietary and confidential.
Source: Bloomberg

**Layout**

Single-slide chart layout on a full-bleed dark slate background. The title sits top-left at about 6% from the top and 3.4% from the left margin, with the yellow subtitle directly beneath it. A line chart occupies the central area, with the y-axis labels at the left edge, the x-axis along the bottom, and a vertical legend stacked at the right, around 86.6% from the left. The copyright line sits bottom-left at about 96% from the top and the source line bottom-right. No slide number is visible.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled 'Generational change in scale' with subtitle 'Employees (000s)'. Y-axis labelled 'Thousands', linear, from 0 (shown as '-') to 150, with gridline labels at 50, 100 and 150; no gridlines are drawn. X-axis runs from 2000 to 2016 with labels at 2000, 2005, 2010 and 2015, and a solid white baseline at zero. Four series: Microsoft as a dotted orange line, Google as a solid steel-blue line, Apple as a solid magenta line, and Facebook as a solid light-green line. Approximate values, none printed on the slide: Microsoft about 39 (2000), 46 (2001), 50 (2002), 55 (2003), 57 (2004), 61 (2005), 70 (2006), 79 (2007), 90 (2008), 92 (2009), 88 (2010), 89 (2011), 93 (2012), 99 (2013), 127 (2014), 118 (2015), 115 (2016). Google from 2004 about 3, 5 (2005), 10 (2006), 17 (2007), 20 (2008), 19 (2009), 24 (2010), 32 (2011), 53 (2012), 47 (2013), 53 (2014), 61 (2015), 71 (2016). Apple from 2000 about 8, 9 (2001), 12 (2002), 13 (2003), 14 (2004), 15 (2005), 18 (2006), 21 (2007), 31 (2008), 34 (2009), 46 (2010), 60 (2011), 73 (2012), 80 (2013), 92 (2014), 109 (2015), 116 (2016). Facebook from 2009 about 1, 2 (2010), 3 (2011), 5 (2012), 6 (2013), 9 (2014), 13 (2015), 17 (2016). Legend at the right, stacked vertically with line-sample swatches matching each series: dotted orange for Microsoft, solid steel blue for Google, solid magenta for Apple, solid green for Facebook. No highlighted points. Lines are about 0.5% of slide height thick. The copyright and source lines are rendered as small images of text.

**Design**

Background: Flat dark slate #40484F covering the full slide. Dark slate background with flat fill and no gradient. Title and axis labels in white, subtitle in amber. Title typeface is a light geometric sans-serif (ProximaNova-Light), about 5.9% of slide height. Subtitle is about 4% of slide height in amber #FCB316. Axis labels and legend are light Helvetica-style sans-serif, about 2.7% to 3.5% of slide height, in white. Series colours: Microsoft orange #D98228 dotted, Google steel blue #6E9EB8, Apple magenta #C2407F, Facebook green #8DC63F; all lines are rounded-cap strokes of uniform width. Axis baseline is a 1px white line. No shadows, corner radii or gradients.

**Layout**

Single-column chart slide with a left-aligned title and subtitle at the top-left margin (about 3.4% from the left edge). The line chart fills the middle and extends to about 81% of the width, with a horizontal x-axis baseline at about 81% of the height. The legend sits in a vertical list at the right (x about 86.6%), and the footer line (copyright at bottom-left, source at bottom-right) sits near the bottom edge at about 95.5% of the height. No slide number is visible.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled 'Engineers, salespeople, retail, logistics…' with subtitle 'Employees (000s)'. Y-axis: 'Thousands' label, ticks at -, 100, 200, 300, 400 (zero shown as '-'); range 0 to 400 thousand employees. X-axis: years 2000 to 2016, labelled 2000, 2005, 2010, 2015 (ticks at 5-year intervals, data points roughly annual). Five series, values are estimates marked about because no values are printed: Microsoft (orange dotted line): about 38 in 2000, about 60 in 2005, about 88 in 2010, about 125 in 2014, about 115 in 2016. Google (steel-blue solid line, starts 2004): about 3 in 2004, about 22 in 2010, about 52 in 2012, about 45 in 2013, about 70 in 2016. Apple (magenta-pink solid line): about 8 in 2000, about 13 in 2005, about 45 in 2010, about 70 in 2012, about 90 in 2014, about 107 in 2015, about 115 in 2016. Facebook (green solid line, starts 2009): about 1 in 2009, about 5 in 2013, about 15 in 2016. Amazon (amber/yellow solid line): about 8 in 2000, about 10 in 2005, about 33 in 2010, about 85 in 2012, about 115 in 2013, about 152 in 2014, about 230 in 2015, about 340 in 2016 (steepest rise, ending highest). Legend at right lists Microsoft (dotted), Google, Apple, Facebook, Amazon, each with a short line swatch in its series colour. No highlighted points. The image-based footer marks are described in elements.

**Design**

Background: Solid flat colour #40484F (dark slate grey). Flat dark slate background #40484F with no gradient. Title in ProximaNova-Light (geometric sans-serif, light weight) at about 5.9% of slide height, white #FFFFFF. Subtitle in ProximaNova-Light at about 4.0% of slide height, amber #FCB316. Axis and legend labels in Helvetica-Light (sans-serif, light weight) at about 2.7% to 3.5% of slide height, white #FFFFFF. Series colours: Microsoft #D98228 dotted, Google steel blue #6E9BB5, Apple magenta #C2437F, Facebook green #8BC34A, Amazon amber #FCB316; lines about 0.5% of slide height thick, round-capped. Baseline is a thin white line. No shadows, corner radii or gradients.

**Layout**

Single full-bleed chart slide with a left-aligned title block at top-left (title at about 3.4% from left, 6% from top) and a subtitle beneath it. The line chart fills the centre and runs to about 81% of the width, with a vertical y-axis label column at the left and a vertical legend list at the right (about 86.6% from left). Footer line sits at about 95.5% from top: copyright at bottom-left and source at bottom-right, both at roughly 3% and 91% from left. No slide number is visible.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled 'LOTS of logistics' with subtitle 'Employees (000s)'. Y-axis: linear, unit thousands of employees, labelled 0 (shown as '-'), 250, 500 and 750 (top of range about 750; the axis label 'Thousands' is positioned vertically at the far left). X-axis: years 2000 to about 2017, tick labels 2000, 2005, 2010, 2015. Series (values are about estimates, as no data labels are printed): Microsoft, dotted orange line, about 40 in 2000, about 60 in 2005, about 90 in 2010, about 120 in 2015 and about 115 in 2016. Google, steel-blue line, starts about 2004 near 0, about 25 in 2010, about 55 in 2012, about 60 in 2015 and about 75 in 2016. Apple, magenta line, about 10 in 2000, about 50 in 2010, about 90 in 2014 and about 115 in 2016. Facebook, green line, starts about 2009 near 0, about 5 in 2012, about 10 in 2014 and about 15 in 2016. Amazon, amber-orange line, about 8 in 2000, about 30 in 2010, about 115 in 2013, about 220 in 2015, about 340 in 2016 and about 600 in 2017, the steepest series and the one that ends highest. No highlighted points. Legend at right, vertical list with a short line swatch before each name: Microsoft (dotted orange), Google (steel blue), Apple (magenta), Facebook (green), Amazon (amber). Two small footer text images and one further small raster element sit at bottom. A filled shape #D98228 lies behind the chart area (x 9.3%, y 71.4%, w 65.7%, h 7.1%) and is not a visible block in the render. Axis baseline is a thin white horizontal line at about 81% from top.

**Design**

Background: Solid flat colour #40484F across the whole slide. Flat dark slate background #40484F. Title and y/x axis labels and legend names in white #FFFFFF, light geometric sans-serif (ProximaNova-Light for title, Helvetica-Light for chart labels), title size about 5.9% of slide height and chart labels about 2.7 to 3.5%. Subtitle and Amazon series in amber #FCB316 (subtitle) and #FCB316 for Amazon line. Series colours: Microsoft #D98228 dotted, Google #6F9DBF, Apple #C4437F, Facebook #7CC05A, Amazon #FCB316. Lines are about 0.5% of slide height thick (Microsoft dotted). Baseline white thin rule. No corner radii, shadows or gradients. Footer text small, about 1.2% of height, white-grey.

**Layout**

Single full-bleed chart slide with a left-aligned title block at the top (title above subtitle) and a full-width stacked area chart below it. The chart's plot area runs from about x 7.4% to 81.1% with the y-axis labels at the left margin and the legend to the right of the plot, stacked top to bottom as Other internet, FB, Google, Print. The footer line sits at bottom left (x 2.9%) and the source line at bottom right (ends near x 95.3%); there is no slide number and no logo. Left margin is about 3.4% of slide width.

**Visuals**

Chart: stacked area chart, no chart title beyond the subtitle 'Global ad revenue ($bn, real)'. Y-axis: linear, labelled 300, 250, 200, 150, 100, 50 and '-' (zero) with gridlines absent; unit $bn real; range 0 to about 300. X-axis: years 1980 to about 2016, labelled every five years from 1980 to 2015, with a thin white baseline at the zero line. Series stacked bottom to top: Print (#6C96B2, bottom layer), Google (#A4621D), FB (#D98228), Other internet (#E8B47E, top layer). Values are not printed; estimates: 1980 total about 110 (all Print); 2000 total about 221 with Print about 211; 2007 total about 236 with Google about 22 and Print about 190; 2009 total about 201 with Print about 140 and Google about 26; 2016 total about 265 with Print about 87, Google about 91, FB about 27, Other internet about 60. Legend at right, white labels, colour swatches for each series. No highlighted points.

**Design**

Background: Solid flat fill #40484F.

### Slide 20

**Text**

Super-evolved organisms?
GAFA seem to have a different mix of character to many tech giants before them
Massively
greater scale
+
Everyone read
the book –
saw Yahoo &
MySpace fail
+
Founder-
controlled
(except Apple)
+
Four, not one
©2017 Andreessen Horowitz. Proprietary and confidential.

**Layout**

Single slide with no grid lines. The title sits top-left at about 6% from the top with a left margin of about 3.4%, and the amber subtitle sits directly beneath it. Four equal circles sit in one horizontal row, centred vertically in the lower-middle of the slide, separated by three grey plus signs at equal spacing. The copyright footer sits bottom-left, and there is no slide number.

**Visuals**

Four solid filled circles of equal size, in a left-to-right row, joined by three plus-sign shapes. Circle 1 is steel blue #6C96B2 with the text 'Massively greater scale'. Circle 2 is magenta #BE4681 with the text 'Everyone read the book – saw Yahoo & MySpace fail'. Circle 3 is lime green #95CA53 with the text 'Founder-controlled (except Apple)'. Circle 4 is dark amber #CB8B03 with the text 'Four, not one'. Between the circles are three plus signs, each a bold cross of about 2.8% width and 5.5% height: the first is grey #8E99A3 and the second and third are light steel blue #A9C4D6. A small image of 215 by 71 pixels sits at about x 79.7%, y 52.0%, inside the orange circle; it shows no visible content distinct from the text and is probably a transparent or hidden graphic. Overlaid at bottom-left is a 305 by 26 pixel image carrying the copyright line. No photographs, charts or tables.

**Design**

Background: Solid flat slate grey #40484F.

### Slide 21

_Long standard legal text on this slide is summarised, not transcribed; its exact words are in the measured list below._

**Text**

But winners always look invulnerable, until they don’t
Microsoft’s share of personal computing unit sales (PCs, Macs, Linux, smartphones & tablets)
100%
75%
50%
25%
0%
2000
2005
2010
2015
©2017 Andreessen Horowitz. Proprietary and confidential.
Source: Gartner, Apple, Google, Microsoft, Nokia, Blackberry, a16z

**Layout**

Single full-bleed chart slide with no grid or columns. The title sits top-left at about 3.4% from the left edge and 6% from the top, with the amber subtitle directly beneath it, both left-aligned on the same left margin. The line chart occupies the middle band from about 24% to 82% of the height, with percentage labels stacked down the left margin and year labels under a horizontal baseline. The copyright line sits bottom-left and the source line bottom-right, both at about 95.5% from the top. No slide number is visible.

**Visuals**

Chart: line chart with no chart title inside the plot. X-axis: calendar years from 2000 to 2016 with labels at 2000, 2005, 2010 and 2015, no units printed. Y-axis: percentage share from 0% to 100%, labels at 0%, 25%, 50%, 75% and 100%. Single series, no legend, no data labels, no highlighted points. Values are not printed, so these are estimates: about 96% from 2000 to 2003, about 96.5% in 2004, about 95.5% in 2005 and 2006, about 93.5% in 2007, about 90% in 2008, about 82% in 2009, about 69% in 2010, about 47% in 2011, about 31% in 2012, about 19.5% in 2013, about 15% in 2014, about 12.5% in 2015 and about 11% in 2016. The line is a single orange stroke about 4 px thick with rounded joins, falling steeply between 2008 and 2013. Bottom horizontal axis line is thin and white. No photographs, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background: Solid flat slate grey #40484F covering the whole slide. Flat dark slate background with no gradient, texture or shadow. Title text is white #FFFFFF in ProximaNova Light, a geometric sans-serif, at about 5.9% of slide height, sentence case, no capitals or letter-spacing. Subtitle is amber #FCB316 in ProximaNova Light at about 4.0% of slide height. Axis labels are white #FFFFFF in Helvetica Light, a neo-grotesque sans-serif, at about 2.7% of slide height for percentages and about 3.5% for years. Footer lines are small white-grey Helvetica-style text at roughly 1.2% of height. Chart line is orange #D9822B. Axis baseline is white #FFFFFF. No corner radii, borders or boxes around content.

**Layout**

Single full-bleed panel with the title and subtitle left-aligned at the top-left margin (about 3.4% from the left edge). A wide line chart fills the middle and runs to about 81% of the width, with its legend stacked at the right in the open space at about 84.5% x. The footer line sits at the bottom left (about 3% margin) and the source credit at the bottom right, both around 95.5% of the height. No slide number is visible.

**Visuals**

Line chart, untitled apart from the slide subtitle 'Annual net revenue ($bn, real)'. Y axis: 0 to 500, labelled every 100 (0, 100, 200, 300, 400, 500), unit $bn real, with a vertical axis label 'Thousands' printed at the far left in very faint small type. X axis: years 1980 to 2016, labelled every five years (1980, 1985, 1990, 1995, 2000, 2005, 2010, 2015), white horizontal baseline at value 0. Series 1, Walmart: steel-blue line (#6B94AB), about 1 in 1980, about 2 in 1982, about 6 in 1985, about 25 in 1990, about 65 in 1993, about 135 in 1998, about 165 in 2000, about 215 in 2001, about 285 in 2004, about 345 in 2006, about 403 in 2008, about 420 in 2010, about 468 in 2012, about 485 in 2014, ending about 485 in 2016. Series 2, Amazon: orange line (#E07F26), starts about 0 in 1995, about 2 in 2000, about 6 in 2004, about 18 in 2008, about 35 in 2010, about 70 in 2013, ending about 135 in 2016. No values are printed on the chart; all values are estimates. Legend at right: blue line swatch with 'Walmart' and orange line swatch with 'Amazon', white labels. Line weight about 0.4% of slide height, round joins. No highlighted points. Footer and source are small images of text.

**Design**

Background: Solid slate grey #40484F covering the whole slide. Flat dark slate background, no gradient or texture. Title in white ProximaNova-Light sans-serif, regular weight, sentence case. Subtitle in amber #FCB316 ProximaNova-Light. Axis labels and legend in white Helvetica-Light sans-serif. Title height about 5.9% of slide height, subtitle about 4.0%, tick labels about 2.7% (x-axis labels about 3.5%). Series colours steel blue #6B94AB and burnt orange #E07F26, lines about 0.4% of slide height thick, no shadows or corner radii.

### Slide 23

**Text**

The winners always look invulnerable, until they don’t
They lose their market, or their market becomes irrelevant
IBM
Microsoft /
Intel /
Nokia
AOL /
MySpace /
Yahoo
GAFA
?
©2017 Andreessen Horowitz. Proprietary and confidential.

**Layout**

Single title block sits top-left at the margin, with an amber subtitle directly beneath it. Five equal circles sit in one horizontal row across the vertical middle of the slide, joined by four grey right-pointing arrows, each circle roughly 14% of slide width and spaced evenly. A small copyright line sits bottom-left. No slide number is visible.

**Visuals**

Five filled circles of equal size in a left-to-right row, each with a centred white label: IBM (blue circle), Microsoft / Intel / Nokia (magenta circle), AOL / MySpace / Yahoo (green circle), GAFA (orange circle), and a question mark (purple circle). Four solid grey (#858D98) block arrows point rightward between adjacent circles, connecting them in sequence. No chart, photograph or logo; the copyright line is a small raster image of text.

**Design**

Background: Solid slate #40484F. Flat dark slate background with no gradient, texture or shadow. Title in white ProximaNova Light (sans-serif, light weight, about 5.9% of slide height). Subtitle in amber #FCB316, same family and weight, about 3.95% of slide height. Circle labels in white ProximaNova Regular, about 3.5% of slide height. Circles have flat fills with no outline, arrows are flat grey block shapes with no outline. Footer copyright is tiny white text on the slate background.

### Slide 24

**Text**

The winners always look invulnerable, until they don’t
They lose their market, or their market becomes irrelevant
IBM
Microsoft / Intel / Nokia
AOL / MySpace / Yahoo
GAFA
?
©2017 Andreessen Horowitz. Proprietary and confidential.

**Layout**

Title sits top-left at about 3.4% from the left edge and 6% from the top, with a light subtitle directly beneath it. Five equal circles sit in one horizontal row across the vertical middle (y about 43% to 68%), evenly spaced with a grey arrow between each adjacent pair. The copyright footer sits bottom-left at about 2.9% from the left edge and 95.5% from the top. There is no slide number.

**Visuals**

Five solid flat circles in one row, left to right: a steel-blue circle (#6C96B2) labelled IBM with a large semi-transparent dark slate X over it; a magenta circle (#BE4681) labelled Microsoft / Intel / Nokia with a semi-transparent dark maroon X over it; a lime-green circle (#95CA53) labelled AOL / MySpace / Yahoo with a semi-transparent dark olive-green X over it; an amber circle (#CB8B03) labelled GAFA with no X; a purple circle (#904CBB) labelled ? with no X. Between each pair of circles is a flat grey right-pointing block arrow (about #838D99), about 4.5% of slide width and 5% of slide height, with no outline. The three X marks are semi-transparent (about 50% opacity) and drawn over the labels, so the label text shows through them. No photographs, logos or icons beyond these.

**Design**

Background: Solid flat colour #40484F (dark slate grey) covering the whole slide. Flat dark slate background (#40484F) with no gradient or texture. Titles and labels are white (#FFFFFF); the subtitle is amber-yellow (#FCB316). Typeface is ProximaNova: Light for the title and subtitle, Regular for the circle labels and the question mark, all sans-serif. Title height is about 5.9% of slide height and body/labels about 3.5%. Circles are flat fills with no outline, no shadow and no gradient. Arrows are flat grey with no outline. The X marks are semi-transparent flat fills. Footer is small white-grey text.

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: A plain line chart with a one-line headline shows how real adoption curves are rarely smooth, using the data itself as the point.
- Review: Slides 7 and 26 are sparse but readable, with large type and good contrast.

The confidentiality footer sits bottom-left at about 95.5% from the top, and the source line sits bottom-right. No slide number is visible.

**Visuals**

Line chart with no legend. Title: none inside the chart area; the slide title and subtitle act as its heading. **Layout**

A single full-bleed chart slide with no grid or columns. The title sits top-left at about 6% from the top, with the amber subtitle directly beneath it, both left-aligned to a margin of about 3.4% of the slide width. The line chart fills the middle of the slide, with its y-axis labels left-aligned near x 3% and its category labels along the bottom of the plot. The copyright line sits bottom-left at about 95.5% from the top, and the source line sits bottom-right, right-aligned to about 96% of the width. There is no slide number.

**Visuals**

Line chart. Title: none inside the chart; the subtitle above it reads 'iPod unit sales (m)'. Y-axis: linear, labelled in units of millions of units, range 0.0 to 15.0, gridlines none, tick labels 0.0, 2.5, 5.0, 7.5, 10.0, 12.5, 15.0. X-axis: time, labelled every six months from Dec-01 to Dec-05 (tick labels Dec-01, Jun-02, Dec-02, Jun-03, Dec-03, Jun-04, Dec-04, Jun-05, Dec-05), with intermediate data points between labels. Single series, drawn as an orange line of about 4 px stroke with round joins, no markers. Values are not printed; approximate values read from the plot are: Dec-01 about 0.05, Jun-02 about 0.02, Dec-02 about 0.15, Jun-03 about 0.2, Dec-03 about 0.7, Jun-04 about 0.8, Dec-04 about 4.5, Jun-05 about 6.1, Dec-05 about 14.0. The last point, at Dec-05, is the highlighted peak, and the line rises steeply from about 6.5 to about 14.0 between the last two points. No legend. The horizontal baseline is a thin white axis line at 0.0. Small raster image of the copyright text, 305 by 26 pixels, white, sits bottom-left. Two small raster images sit bottom-right: one 112 by 52 pixels and one 90 by 52 pixels, both part of the 'Source: Apple' line rendering, with no distinct visible content.

**Design**

Background: Solid slate grey #40484F covering the full slide. Flat dark slate background, no gradient, no texture. Title in a light geometric sans-serif (Proxima Nova Light style), weight light, white #FFFFFF, large at about 5.9% of slide height for cap height and about 6% for line height. Subtitle in the same light sans, amber #FCB316, about 4% of slide height. Chart axis labels in a Helvetica Light style sans-serif, white #FFFFFF, about 2.7% of slide height for the y-axis labels and about 3.5% for the x-axis labels. Chart line orange #D97F26. Baseline white #FFFFFF, about 1 px thin. Footer and source text are small, light sans-serif, white or pale grey, about 1.3% of slide height. No shadows, no rounded corners, no shapes other than the line and baseline.

### Slide 28

**Text**

Three phases of creation
Get the tech to
work at all
Find product-market fit
Pour on rocket fuel
©2017 Andreessen Horowitz. Proprietary and confidential.

**Layout**

Title sits top-left at about 6% from the top with a left margin of about 3.4%. Three equal-width chevron panels (each about 35.7% wide, 66.4% tall) sit in one row starting at x 1.8% and spanning to about 94.7%, with their tops at 23.6% and bottoms at about 90%, separated by background-coloured gaps. Each panel's label is centred near its top. The copyright footer sits bottom-left at about 95.5%. No slide number is visible.

**Visuals**

One line chart drawn without axes, title, legend, or printed values. It is a single orange polyline with rounded joins, about 4 px thick on a 2000 px-wide slide. It starts at about x 34.3%, y 82% (about 930 px of 1125 px height) and runs nearly flat with slight dips through about x 45% to 58%, then rises gently to about x 69% at y 80%, then steepens sharply. It climbs through about x 73% (y 67%), x 76% (y 60%), and x 80% (y 55%), then kinks upward to about x 86% (y 60%) and ends in a near-vertical stroke at about x 89.5%, y 32%. All values are estimates; none are printed. Three chevron panels form the other visual, described in the elements list. The footer is a white wordmark-style text image (305 by 26 px) reading the copyright line.

**Design**

Background: Solid slate colour #40484F, full bleed. Dark slate background #40484F. Chevron panels are a darker charcoal #30363B with no corner radius, shadow, or gradient, and the gaps between them show the background colour. Titles and panel labels are white #FFFFFF, with the panel labels in bold. The accent is an orange line about #D98326. Typefaces are Proxima Nova Light for the title and Proxima Nova Bold for the panel labels, both sans-serif. The title is about 5.9% of slide height and the panel labels about 3.5%. The footer is a small regular-weight sans-serif in white.

Solid fill #30363B, no outline, no corner radius, no shadow. Sits on the background; the chevron gap to panel 2 is #40484F.)

- shape at x 59, y 23.6, w 35.7, h 66.4 [#30363B]: Chevron panel 3 (right), no text inside (Chevron/pentagon shape with notched left edge matching panel 2's tip and a right-pointing tip at about x 94.7%. Proprietary and confidential.

**Layout**

Two magazine cover images sit side by side in the centre of the slide, both about 27 percent of slide width and 62.5 percent of slide height, with their tops aligned at 26.8 percent from the top. The title sits top-left at 6.1 percent from the top and 3.4 percent from the left, left-aligned on one line. A small copyright footer image sits bottom-left at 95.5 percent from the top and 2.9 percent from the left. No slide number is shown.

**Visuals**

Two magazine cover photographs, shown as flat rectangular images with no border or shadow. Left: The Economist cover (400 by 526 pixels) with a sepia-gold Art Deco illustration of a metallic robot head with a helmet-like headpiece, bust and shoulders, framed by vertical golden pillars and diagonal light beams; a red rectangular masthead reading 'The Economist' in white serif type at upper left; a column of white sans-serif cover lines at upper right; a large white bold headline 'March of the machines' across the middle; a small white caps line 'A SPECIAL REPORT ON ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE' below it; dominant colours ochre, gold and dark brown. Right: Bloomberg Businessweek cover (398 by 525 pixels) on a black background showing a three-dimensional cartoon robot built from pastel blue, teal, pink and orange parts, with a glass cylinder containing a pink brain on its head, a large paper-clip body with a googly eye, and legs standing on a cyan perspective grid floor; green bold masthead 'Bloomberg Businessweek' at upper left (the 'Business' line partly covered by the robot's head), pink headline 'Spawn of Clippy' at left and small yellow-green text block below it; dominant colours black, cyan, pastel blue and pink. Both covers are full-colour, uncropped and unlit by any overlay. Behind each cover, a dark charcoal rectangle (#30363B) peeks out at the cover's left and right edges, about 2 percent of slide width wide beyond the image edges, at the vertical middle of the covers.

**Design**

Background: Solid slate grey #40484F. Flat, minimal dark-grey slide. Background solid charcoal-slate #40484F. Title in a light geometric sans-serif (Proxima Nova Light), white #FFFFFF, regular weight, about 5.9 percent of slide height, sentence case, no accent colour. No body text in the layout apart from the cover content. Shapes are plain rectangles with square corners, no shadows, no gradients, no outlines. The darker rectangles #30363B are tabs behind the covers. Footer copyright in small light grey-white sans-serif.

### Slide 32

**Text**

Unhelpful ways to
talk about AI

Data is the new oil!
Google has all the data!
China will have all the AI!
AI will kill us!
AI will take all the jobs!

Calling it ‘AI’

©2017 Andreessen Horowitz. Proprietary and confidential.

**Layout**

Two-column split with a dark grey left panel about 35% of the slide width and a full-height photograph filling the right 65%. The title sits top-left at about 6% from the top, left-aligned at about 3.4% from the left edge, in two lines. The body text is left-aligned in a block starting at about 37% height, with a blank line before the final line. The copyright footer sits bottom-left at about 95.5% from the top. No slide number.

**Visuals**

One photograph in the right 65% of the slide, full-bleed to the top, bottom and right edges, butted against the grey panel on its left edge. Subject: a tall black rectangular monolith standing upright on a rocky, sandy desert floor, with three dark furry apes around it: one crouched on a rock ledge at the upper right, one small and seated in the middle distance left of the monolith, and one standing upright in the foreground lower centre. A dark rock outcrop frames the left edge and a large dark shape frames the top right corner. Action: the apes are gathered near the monolith at dusk. Composition: monolith is near-vertical and centred slightly left of middle, filling most of the image height; horizon sits about two-thirds up. Lighting: low warm sun behind the scene, backlit sky with glowing white and yellow centre fading to orange and red-brown clouds. Dominant colours: sky orange #E8A060 and cream #F5E6C8, rock tan #8A7560, monolith black #1E1E22, apes near-black #15120F. Cropped to fill the box, not boxed, no overlay or tint. No logos or icons on the slide.

**Design**

Background: Flat white #FFFFFF behind the slide, with a dark slate panel #40484F covering the left 35% and a photograph covering the right 65%. Two flat colour zones with no gradients, shadows or rounded corners. Left panel is dark slate grey. Title is light-weight geometric sans-serif (Proxima Nova Light), white, sized about 5.9% of slide height, lowercase except capital A and I in AI. Body text is regular-weight Proxima Nova, white, about 4.4% of slide height, with tight line spacing of about 1.1 and generous space between the title and body. Footer is a small light sans-serif in pale grey-white, about 1% of slide height. Accents: none beyond the photograph.

Text is left-aligned at about 3.4% left margin; no slide number is visible.

One scatter plot (t-SNE style cluster map) of grocery products from Instacart, with no printed title, no axis labels, no axis values and no axis units. Plot area is a light grey #EBEBEB square with white gridlines, spanning about x 38 to 82.7 and y 10.7 to 89.2. About 25 coloured point clusters of varying density; points are small filled circles of roughly 1 to 1.5% of slide width. Visible clusters: pink (Produce/Popular/Snacks hues) cluster at top centre-right, large pink cluster at top-left-centre, blue cluster at upper-left (Meat & Seafood/International hues), olive-green cluster at upper-centre, green cluster at upper-right, orange/brown cluster in centre-left, teal cluster (Healthy Eating hue) in lower-centre, lavender/purple cluster (Pantry and Personal Care hues) at right, dense green cluster at right edge, pink clusters at bottom-left and bottom-centre, and scattered points throughout. No highlighted points. Legend at right of plot: 27 entries, each a small coloured dot in a light grey square swatch followed by a label in dark grey sans-serif, listed top to bottom as Babies (pink-red), Back to School (orange-red), Bakery (orange), Beverages (dark orange), Breakfast (gold), Bulk (olive), Canned Goods (olive green), Dairy & Eggs (green), Deli (green), Dry Goods & Pasta (green), Floral (green), Fromagerie (teal-green), Frozen (teal-green), Healthy Eating (teal), Holidays (cyan), Household (sky blue), International (blue), Meat & Seafood (blue), Olives, Gourmet Cheese, Salads (blue), Pantry (lavender), Personal Care (violet), Pets (violet), Popular (magenta), Produce (pink), Snacks (pink). Legend spans about x 83.9 to 98 and y 15 to 87. Source line 'Source: Instacart' in light grey at bottom-right. Copyright line is in the footer on the dark panel.

Dark slate panel #40484F on the left 35% with a plain white #FFFFFF area on the right; no gradients, shadows or rules. Title and body text are white #FFFFFF in a geometric humanist sans-serif (Proxima Nova), title in Light weight and body in Regular weight, both in sentence case. Footer copyright is small, about 1.5% of slide height, white. Source line is light grey #B0B0B0 small sans-serif. ### Slide 36

**Text**

Cat pictures?
©2017 Andreessen Horowitz. Proprietary and confidential.

**Layout**

Two-column split with a full-height dark slate panel occupying the left 35% of the slide and a full-bleed photograph filling the right 65%. The title sits top-left inside the panel, left-aligned at about 3.4% from the left edge and about 6.4% from the top. The footer runs along the bottom of the dark panel: the copyright line starts at about 2.9% from the left and the source credit sits to its right, ending near the panel's right edge at about 34%. No slide number is visible. The left panel leaves the lower-left area below the title empty.

**Visuals**

One photograph, full-bleed on the right 65% of the slide and cropped by the slide edges on the right, top and bottom. Subject: two young kittens sitting on a beige woven or knitted cat tree or basket in the lower foreground. On the left is a calico kitten with orange, black and white fur, a white chest and white front paws, with blue-green eyes, looking upward and slightly off to the left. On the right is a fluffy black kitten with grey-green eyes, looking towards the viewer. Composition: the two kittens fill the lower two-thirds of the frame, with their heads near the upper third and the basket edge running across the bottom. Background: a soft, out-of-focus pale grey wall, giving a shallow depth of field. Lighting: soft, diffuse natural daylight from the front-left. Dominant colours: warm tan/beige of the basket, pale grey wall, orange and black of the calico kitten, and black of the second kitten. No overlay or tint is applied. Not boxed; the image runs to the slide edge.

**Design**

Background is a flat dark slate panel (#40484F) on the left 35% with the photograph on the right. The title is white (#FFFFFF) in a light geometric sans-serif, recognisable as Proxima Nova Light, set in regular weight with no italics or capitals, at about 5.9% of slide height (cap height about 4.2%). Footer text is small, roughly 1.3% of slide height, in a light sans-serif similar to Proxima Nova Regular, colour near white (#E6E8EA) for the copyright and source line. No accent colours, shapes, rules or shadows are present; the only hard edge is the boundary between the slate panel and the photograph.

Both images are cut out (no crop shape), unboxed, lit evenly by studio light, with dominant colours black, silver and white.

### Slide 39

**Text**

There’s no such
thing as ‘data’

clothes - a voice

network intrusion.

Source: Siemens

**Layout**

Two-column split slide. A full-height dark slate panel fills the left 35% of the slide and holds the title and three body paragraphs left-aligned at about 3.4% from the left edge. The right 65% is white and holds two product photographs side by side, roughly vertically centred and bottom-aligned at about 82% of slide height. The title sits at the top of the left panel at about 6.4% from the top. The footer runs along the bottom of the left panel (copyright at left, 'Source: Siemens' at right) at about 95.5% from the top. No slide number is visible.

**Visuals**

Two product photographs of Siemens home appliances, shown as white cut-out products with no background. Left: a freestanding white dishwasher with its door dropped open toward the viewer, showing plates, bowls and cutlery in the upper and lower racks, a black control panel with a small digital display on the door top edge, and a light grey worktop-style top. Right: a white front-loading washing machine with a brushed silver chrome door ring and round drum window showing laundry inside, a Siemens wordmark above the control panel, a round silver dial and a small digital display at upper right. Both products are viewed from a three-quarter front angle, uncropped, shown at equal size, with a soft grey floor shadow beneath each. Lighting is even studio light. Dominant colours are white, light grey and chrome silver. Neither photo has an overlay or tint, and neither is boxed or full-bleed.

**Design**

Background is white. The left panel is flat dark slate with no gradient, shadow or border. The title is a light-weight geometric sans-serif (ProximaNova-Light style) in white, set large at about 5.9% of slide height per line with tight leading. Body text is a regular-weight geometric sans-serif (ProximaNova-Regular style) in white at about 3.95% of slide height, with line spacing of about 4.4% of slide height and extra space between paragraphs. Footer text is small light-grey sans-serif. There are no rules, shapes with rounded corners, icons or accent colours.

### Slide 46

**Text**

Mechanised legs
Ilya Repin, ‘Barge-haulers on the Volga’, 1873
©2017 Andreessen Horowitz. Proprietary and confidential.

**Layout**

A dark slate header band fills the top 21.9% of the slide, with the title and an amber subtitle left-aligned at about 3.4% from the left edge. Below it, a full-bleed horizontal painting fills the remaining 78.1% edge to edge. A small copyright line sits in the bottom-left corner over the painting. There is no slide number.

**Visuals**

Photograph-like reproduction of Ilya Repin's oil painting 'Barge-haulers on the Volga' (1873), full-bleed, 1500 by 659 px, cropped at the left and right edges. Subject: about ten barefoot or booted men in ragged clothing lean forward in a line, each harnessed by a broad dark strap to a towing rope running left to right. A bearded man with a pipe stands among them and a young boy in a pink shirt stands in the centre looking to the right. On the right, a two-masted sailing barge with rigging and a small red-clad crew sits on calm water with a flag at its masthead. A small sailboat is far left. In the foreground, a sandbank with a wicker basket, a folded sack and a fallen stick. Lighting is soft, warm, low-angle daylight. Dominant colours are ochre sand about #C8A46E, pale blue-grey sky about #B9CCD8, dark olive and brown clothing about #3D4632, and warm pink about #D9907F. Composition is horizontal with the figures in the left-centre band and the barge at right. A faint date and signature appear bottom right. A small image of the copyright text sits over the sand, bottom left, rendered in white at reduced opacity.

**Design**

Background: Dark slate #40484F header band across the top, with the full-bleed painting covering the lower part of the slide. Header band is dark slate #40484F. Title is white #FFFFFF in ProximaNova Light, a geometric sans-serif with light weight, at about 5.9% of slide height. Subtitle is amber #FCB316 in ProximaNova Light at about 3.95% of slide height. Copyright line is small white sans-serif. No body text, bullets, rules, shapes, shadows or gradients.

### Slide 47

**Text**

Mechanised arms
Ford production line, 1920s
©2017 Andreessen Horowitz. Proprietary and confidential.

**Layout**

A full-width dark header band occupies the top 21.9% of the slide, with the title and subtitle left-aligned at about 3.4% from the left edge, stacked in the upper-left of the band. A black-and-white photograph fills the full width of the lower 78.1% of the slide, edge to edge with no margin. A small copyright line sits bottom-left over the photograph at about 2.9% from the left edge. No slide number is visible.

**Visuals**

One photograph, full-bleed across the lower part of the slide and running off the left, right and bottom edges. Subject: interior of the Ford production line in the 1920s. A long aisle recedes from the foreground toward the vanishing point at the upper left-centre. Along the right, workers in work clothes and flat caps stand at assembly stations with car chassis and engine components, one worker in the foreground leaning over a chassis. Steering wheels hang from overhead rails and tall racks of parts bins line the right side, with crates of components and stacked frame parts. Left side shows a white wall with pipes and hanging equipment, and stacked tyres near the wall. Composition is a deep one-point perspective shot from an elevated viewpoint, with the subject occupying most of the frame. Lighting is diffuse overhead industrial light, producing soft grey tones and no strong shadows. Dominant colours are greyscale: black, charcoal and light grey, with no colour. The image is cropped at the bottom and sides, boxed to the lower area, with no overlay or tint. A small white copyright line overlays the lower-left of the photograph.

Subtitle is amber-yellow (#FCB316) in the same light sans-serif at about 4.0% of slide height. Copyright line is small white sans-serif at a size well under 2% of slide height, with reduced visibility over the photograph. Accent colour is the amber subtitle. Mechanised arithmetic
Billy Wilder, ‘The Apartment’, 1960
©2017 Andreessen Horowitz. Proprietary and confidential.

**Visuals**

One full-bleed black-and-white photograph of a large 1960s open-plan office, seen in one-point perspective from a raised viewpoint looking down the length of the room. Rows of metal desks run towards a vanishing point at the centre, with men and women seated at desks, typing on manual and electric typewriters and operating calculating machines, while others stand and walk between the rows. The ceiling is a grid of rectangular fluorescent light panels in diagonal coffers, and concrete columns line the aisles. Lighting is flat, bright and overhead, with the foreground brighter at the bottom. Dominant colours are greys, black and white. The photograph is cropped at the slide edges and is full-bleed beneath the header band. A small white copyright line overlays the lower-left corner of the photograph.

Computer
10 year old
15 year old

Four solid filled circles of equal size (each about 18.1% wide by 32.2% high of the slide) with their labels centred inside: an orange circle reading Computer, a steel-blue circle reading 10 year old, a green circle reading 15 year old, and a purple circle reading ?. Three grey right-pointing block arrows sit between adjacent circles, each pointing from the circle on its left to the next circle, showing a left-to-right sequence. Arrow fill is about #848E9A with no outline. The bottom-left element is a small placed image (305 by 26 pixels) carrying the copyright text in light grey-white. No photographs, charts or icons.

### Slide 51

_Long standard legal text on this slide is summarised, not transcribed; its exact words are in the measured list below._

**Text**

Automated recommendation? / How does ecommerce change when computers can see what you like? / ©2017 Andreessen Horowitz. Proprietary and confidential. / Source: Pinterest / On screenshot: Pin it / Like / Visit Site / Send / Share / Similar Looks / Click the red dot to see similar looks. / MICHAEL KORS (embossed on the bag, faint and only partly legible)

**Layout**

Two-column split slide with no legal text. The left 35% of the slide is a full-height dark slate panel holding the title at top-left (x 3.4%, y 6.4%), a four-line body block starting at y 37%, and a footer line near the bottom edge. The right 65% is a white field holding one large Pinterest-style web screenshot, full-height and flush to the top and bottom edges, with its own toolbar and a 'Similar Looks' thumbnail grid on its right side. Left margin is about 3.4% of slide width, all text is left-aligned, and there is no slide number visible.

**Visuals**

One photograph-style screenshot, placed as a single image covering x 40.3% to 96% and full slide height (498 by 503 pixels, shown with its top edge at the slide top). Its left part is a web toolbar strip across the top with a red Pin it button at the left (red #E60023 with white lettering), then grey pill buttons labelled Like (heart icon), Visit Site (with a small icon), and at the right Send and Share (Facebook-style f icon), on a light grey #F0F0F0 bar. Below the toolbar is a large colour photo of a woman in dark navy trousers seen from the waist down, holding an orange-red leather tote with gold hardware and a gold Michael Kors-style charm; her beige heeled sandals stand on grey paving stones; the upper part is cropped by the frame and her hand grips the handle at top; background is a pale grey wall and a dark grey doorway at the right. Lighting is soft daylight, and the dominant colours are orange-red #E85A2A, navy #1E2430 and grey #8A8E93. Two small red hotspot dots (about #E8402A with white rings) sit on the bag near its centre and on the sandal near the bottom; above the central dot is a white tooltip box with the text Click the red dot to see similar looks. The photo is boxed inside the screenshot, not full-bleed. To its right, a Similar Looks panel has a small thumbnail at top-left and a two-column grid of about 19 tote-bag thumbnails, each on a white card with a thin light-grey border: row 1 a small multi-tile collage (orange, peach, pink, grey) and a coral-red tote; row 2 an orange tote and a woman in black top holding a tan tote; row 3 a bright orange tote and a tan tote; row 4 a teal tote and a tan tote; row 5 a brown tote and a camel tote; row 6 a brown tote and a pink-coral tote; row 7 a brown tote and a green partial tote; row 8 a pink-coral tote at the bottom. Thumbnails are cropped to a square-ish frame and the last row is cut off at the bottom edge.

**Design**

Background: Solid dark slate #40484F on the left 35% and solid white #FFFFFF on the right 65%.

©2017 Andreessen Horowitz. Proprietary and confidential.
